Let's understand SOTA algorithm: Given f (nonnegative) with sum normalized S=√(2n). Let M=||f*f||∞. Solve LP: max sum g_j subject to g_j≥0 and ||f*g||∞ ≤ M. Then rescale g to sum S and update f←(1-t)f + t g. This is like finding a feasible direction that has larger sum under convolution bound M. Why does it reduce M? If f itself is feasible (f*f≤M), max sum ≥ sum f =S. If strict, can scale up f; after rescale, new M maybe lower. It is projection onto feasible set? Repeating converges to Pareto optimal where no feasible nonnegative g has larger sum under same M, i.e. at M boundary. This is a kind of Chebyshev center? It might find low max conv for fixed sum.

Primal:

g_j ≥0.

y_k ≥0.
where if valid k. Here A_{k,j}=a_{k-j}. Dual coefficients same. We need find nonnegative weights y over lags such that weighted sum of a around each j ≥1, minimizing M sum y. SOTA algorithm details more carefully:
Given f normalized sum S. M=||f*f||∞. Solve LP: maximize sum g subject to g≥0 and (f*g)_k≤M. Note f*g is cross-convolution between fixed f and variable g, not g*g. This is a linear constraint. The optimal g has larger sum if current f not optimal. Then rescale to S and update f ← (1-t)f+t g. Why nonincreasing M? If g sum larger and f*g≤M, then g is in feasible set under f's M. For the update, we need f_new*f_new? There may be a theorem: It's an instance of duality for rank-one? Let's trust.

We can implement this algorithm even if not full optimum; top active constraints. It is a block coordinate descent: For fixed f, choose best g in polytope P_M={g≥0: f*g≤M}, then move f toward it. This is like Frank-Wolfe on nonconvex objective? It might converge.

LP building rows:
```
def build_active_matrix(f, row_idx):
    n=f.size; L=len(row_idx); A=np.zeros((L,n))
    for r,k in enumerate(row_idx):
       s = max(0, k - (n-1)); e = min(n-1, k)
       # j from s to e coefficient f[k-j]
       # f indices from k-e to k-s inclusive
       A[r, s:e+1] = f[k-e : k-s+1][::-1]
    return A
```

```
def support_gradient(f, lam):
   n=f.size; B=np.zeros(n)
   for k, wk in enumerate(lam):
      if wk==0: continue
      s=max(0,k-(n-1)); e=min(n-1,k)
      B[s:e+1] += wk * f[k-e : k-s+1][::-1]
   return B
```
This is same matrix-vector product. Good.

Let's design `linprog_safe` top-level:
```
def solve_lp(f, M, row_idx, rng, use_highs=True, seed_eps=1e-4):
   n=f.size; L=len(row_idx)
   A=build_matrix(f,row_idx)
   b=np.full(L, M)
   r=rng.random(n)*seed_eps
   c=-(1.0 + r)
   bounds = [(0.0, 1000.0)]*n  # list large but okay; for n=2048 2048 tuples
   try: method='highs'
   except: method='interior-point'
   res=linprog(c,A_ub=A,b_ub=b,bounds=bounds,method=method,options={'presolve':True})
   if res.success: return res.x
   else: maybe try without random/interior
```

Implement `convolve_full` and `max_conv`.
```
def convolve_full(a,b=None):
   n=len(a)
   L=2*n-1
   nfft=1
   while nfft<L: nfft<<=1
   fa=np.fft.rfft(a,nfft)
   if b is None: fb=fa
   else: fb=np.fft.rfft(b,nfft)
   c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fb,nfft)[:L]
   c[np.abs(c)<1e-12]=0
   return c
```
For n=1024, nfft=2048. For many calls, computing nfft each time overhead small. Could use passed nfft but fine.

s=a.sum()
if s <= 0: return inf
c=convolve_full(a)
return 2*a.size*np.max(c)/(s*s)
```

def normalized_M(f):

   return f, np.max(convolve_full(f))

`build_matrix` top-level:
```
def build_active_matrix(f, row_idx):
   n=f.size; L=row_idx.size; A=np.zeros((L,n), dtype=np.float64)
   for r,k in enumerate(row_idx):
      s = max(0, int(k) - (n-1)); e = min(n-1, int(k))
      if s <= e:
          A[r, s:e+1] = f[int(k)-e : int(k)-s+1][::-1]
   return A
```
Use Python list from row_idx; if row_idx np array, enumerate.

`choose_active_rows(c, n, L, rng, frac_top=0.7)`:
```
K=2*n-1
idx=np.arange(K)
# top by c
top_n = int(L*frac_top)
top = np.argsort(c)[::-1][:top_n]
rem = L-top_n
# random weighted by exp((c-M)/temp)
M=np.max(c)
temp=max(1e-12, (M - np.percentile(c,50))*0.5 + 1e-12)
probs=np.exp((c-M)/temp)
probs/=probs.sum() if >0 else uniform
rand_idx=rng.choice(K, rem, replace=False, p=probs)
return np.unique(np.concatenate((top,rand_idx)))[:L]
```
For L maybe >K, use all.

SOTA run top-level:
```
def sota_run(f0, max_time, deadline, full_limit=900, active_L=384, rng=None, verbose=True):
   if rng is None: rng=np.random.default_rng()
   n=f0.size
   S=np.sqrt(2.0*n)
   f=np.asarray(f0,float).copy()
   f=np.clip(f,0,1000.0)
   if f.sum()<=0: return f0, inf
   f=f*S/f.sum()
   best=f.copy(); bestM=np.max(convolve_full(f))
   it=0; no_improve=0; M=bestM
   t_base=0.2
   while time.monotonic()<max_time and time.monotonic()<deadline:
      c=convolve_full(f); M=float(np.max(c))
      if M < bestM - 1e-12: best=f.copy(); bestM=M; no_improve=0
      else: no_improve += 1
      if no_improve>20: perturb f; if score > bestM*1.1 reset best; no_improve=0
      if n <= full_limit:
          row_idx=np.arange(2*n-1)
      else:
          row_idx=choose_active_rows(c,n,min(active_L,2*n-1),rng)
      # try M factors
      improved=False
      for mf in [1.0,0.95,0.9,0.85]:
          A=build_active_matrix(f,row_idx)
          g=solve_lp(f, M*mf, A, rng)
          if g is None: continue
          sg=g.sum()
          if sg <= 1.01*S: continue
          # safe ratio
          fg=convolve_full(f,g)
          maxfg=np.max(fg)
          if maxfg > (M/S)*sg*(1+1e-7):
              # if using mf<1, condition with M factor? Use M actual.
              continue
          g_norm=g*(S/sg)
          # line search
          best_tau=-1; best_cand=None; best_cm=M
          taus=[0.05,0.1,0.2,0.35,0.5,0.75,1.0]
          for tau in taus:
             cand=(1-tau)*f+tau*g_norm
             cand=np.clip(cand,0,None)
             s=cand.sum()
             if s<=0: continue
             cand=cand*(S/s)
             cm=float(np.max(convolve_full(cand)))
             if cm < best_cm - 1e-10:
                 best_cm=cm; best_tau=tau; best_cand=cand
          if best_cand is not None:
              f=best_cand; M=best_cm; improved=True
              if M < bestM: best=f.copy(); bestM=M
              no_improve=0
              break
      if not improved:
          # small gradient step maybe
          grad_step = multiplicative_step(f,n,rng)
          if grad_step is not None and score < M:
             f=grad_step; M=...; if M<bestM update
          else: perturb if no_improve
   return best,bestM
```
But `solve_lp` taking prebuilt A and M. It also needs random objective.

   r=rng.random(n)
   c=-(1.0 + 1e-4*r)
   b=np.full(A.shape[0], M)
   res=linprog(c,A_ub=A,b_ub=b,bounds=(0.0,1000.0),method='highs',options={'presolve':True, 'dual_feasibility_tolerance':1e-7, 'primal_feasibility_tolerance':1e-7})
   if res.success and res.x is not None: return np.asarray(res.x)

Multiplicative step:
```
def multiplicative_step(f,n,rng):
   c=convolve_full(f); M=max
   temp = max(1e-12, (M - np.percentile(c,90))*0.2 + 1e-12)
   w=np.exp((c-M)/temp)
   # randomize sample lags: take K weights, multiply by (0.5+rng.rand(K))? maybe
   if rng.random()<0.5:
       w *= 0.5 + rng.random(c.size)
   w_sum=w.sum(); if w_sum==0 return None
   w=w/w_sum
   B=zeros(n)
   for k,wk in enumerate(w): if wk>1e-12: slice...
   # update
   Bc=B-B.mean()
   std=Bc.std()
   if std<1e-15: return None
   for eta in [0.5/std, 1.0/std, 2.0/std]:
      step=np.exp(-eta*Bc)
      step=np.clip(step,1e-12,None)
      cand=f*step
      cand=np.clip(cand,0,1000)
      if cand.sum()<=0: continue
      cand*=S/cand.sum()
      cm=max(conv(cand))
      if cm < fM -1e-10: return cand
   return None
```
Could be expensive each call (loop over K). Use only every few SOTA iterations.

Perturbation:
```
def random_perturb(f,scale=0.15):
  n=f.size
  cand=f.copy()
  width=int(rng.integers(max(2,n//100), max(3,n//5)))
  start=int(rng.integers(0,n-width+1))
  cand[start:start+width] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0,scale))
  # sometimes mirror with prob
  if rng.random()<0.3: cand=0.5*(cand+cand[::-1])
  # floor
  cand=np.clip(cand,0,1000)
  if cand.sum()<=0: cand=f.copy()
  return cand
```

Step generation function:
```
def make_step_sequence(n, frac, height):
   frac=np.clip(frac,1e-8,None); frac/=frac.sum()
   h=np.clip(height,1e-12,None)
   # approximate mean 1 by h/mean? Not needed if normalize later
   cum=np.concatenate(([0], np.cumsum(frac)))*n
   idx=np.searchsorted(cum, np.arange(n), side='right')
   idx=np.minimum(idx, len(height)-1)
   a=height[idx]
   if a.sum()==0: a=np.ones(n)
   return a
```
Random height patterns:
```
def random_heights(K, rng, style):
   if style==0: ones
   elif style==1: exp(rng.uniform(-0.5,0.5)*np.arange(K))
   elif style==2: 1+rng.uniform(0,1)*np.sin(pi*(i+1)/(K+1))
   elif style==3: lognormal(0, rng uniform 0.1,0.8)
```

For each height variable, block mass derivative B_m = ∑_{i in block m} B_i, M_m mass fraction. Update h_m *= exp(-eta*(B_m/M_m? - mean_b?)). This is exactly gradient for logsumexp but with.max. ```
def optimize_step_simple(frac, n, rng, iters=2000):
  idx=...

```

```

Algorithm `refine_step(frac, n, rng, steps=300)`:
- idx and counts. a = make_step with random initial heights.
- S=√(2n). normalize.
- For it in range(steps): c=conv, M; weights softmax c; B=support_gradient; `a *= np.exp(-eta*(B - B.mean()))`; clip; block_avg: assign each block its mean; normalize; if M improves best_store. Use eta adapt 1/(B.std()+1). Maybe also try reversal initial.
- Return best.
This is essentially full-sequence gradient but projected back to step after each step, which smooths. It may find good step patterns without explicit optimizer.

Initial heights: low-high, high-low, uniform, random.

Implement `compute_B_fft(f, lam)`:
We need B_i =∑_j f_j lam_{i+j} for i=0..n-1. Use convolution correlation.
Let Lw=2n-1. Let L = next power of two >= (n-1)+Lw+1 = 3n-2? For cross-correlation nonwrap, length at least n+Lw-1 =3n-2. Use `P = rfft(f, L)`, `Q = rfft(lam, L)`. Correlation C_s = ∑ f_j lam_{j+s} for s from -(n-1) to Lw-n? Standard: `corr = ifft(conj(P)*Q)` gives circular correlation c[m]=∑ f[j] lam[j+m]? Need verify. In numpy, `np.correlate(f, lam, mode='full')` returns `c[k] = ∑ f[n] conj(lam[n-k])`? Let's test mentally: For sequences, `np.correlate([a],[b])`: `full` length n+m-1: output[k] = ∑ a_x b_{x+k} or b? According docs: `c_k = ∑ n a_{n+k} conj(b_n)`. For real, `c[k]=∑ a[n+k] b[n]`. That's ∑ a_j b[j-k]? If k positive, a shifted. We want ∑ f_j lam[j+s]. This is correlation with s. It is `np.correlate(lam, f, mode='full')`? Let's derive using DFT: linear correlation `C_s = ∑_x f_x w_{x+s}`. Let P=FFT(f), W=FFT(w). The DFT of C_s (as sequence indexed s) is conj(P)*W? For cross-correlation, `np.correlate(f,w,'full')[offset]`. To get s≥0, `corr_full = np.correlate(w, f, 'full')`? Example n=2: f=[f0,f1], w=[w0,w1,w2]. B_0=f0w0+f1w1. B_1=f0w1+f1w2.
`np.correlate(w, f, 'full')`: output length 3? Actually full of w(3) and f(2) length 4? Define `corr[k]=∑ w[n+k] f[n]`. k=0: sum w[n]f[n]=f0w0+f1w1 ->B0. k=1: sum w[n+1]f[n]=f0w1+f1w2 ->B1. Good. So B = first n elements of `np.correlate(w, f, full)`. In FFT: `ifft(FFT(w) * conj(FFT(f)))` maybe. Check: Circular correlation of w and f `∑ w[n+s]f[n]` corresponds to `ifft(W_dev * conj(F_dev))` with proper padding. So use `Bfull=np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(lam,L)*np.conj(np.fft.rfft(f,L)),L)`? Wait if multiply W * conj(F), inverse gives cross-correlation? For DFT, convolution theorem: `ifft(FFT(a)*FFT(b)) = a*b`? Actually DFT: IFFT(FFT(a)*FFT(b)) = circular convolution a*b. Correlation is convolution with reversed. Multiplying W*conj(F) gives circular correlation? Standard: `corr = ifft(conj(F) * W)` for `∑ f[n] w[n+m]`. Let's check with example using small not possible but known: autocorrelation `ifft(FFT(x)*conj(FFT(x)))` gives circular autocorrelation where c[k]=∑ x[n+k] conj(x[n]). So for cross, `ifft(FFT(w)*conj(FFT(f)))` yields c[k]=∑ w[n+k] conj(f[n]) -> B_k. Good. Thus `B = irfft(rfft(lam,L)*np.conj(rfft(f,L)), L)`. Need length L≥ n+Lw-1 = 3n-2. Take first n.
Test real. Good.

```
L=1; while L < 3*n-2: L<<=1
return np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(lam,L)*np.conj(np.fft.rfft(f,L)),L)[:n]
```

def next_pow2(m):
    p=1
    while p<m: p <<=1
    return p
```

`make_two_block`:
```
def make_two_block(n, p, reverse=False):
    p = min(max(float(p),0.01),0.49)
    left_count = int(round(p*n))
    right_count = n-left_count
    a_left = 1/(1+p)
    a_right = 1/((1+p)*(1-p))
    arr = np.empty(n)
    if not reverse:
       arr[:left_count]=a_left; arr[left_count:]=a_right
    else:
       arr[:right_count]=a_right? Wait reversed means low first? We can swap.
```
The optimal p assumes left length p (<0.5) with low height a, right length 1-p high b. Reverse means high length p? Equivalent left length p high a? We can just create low-front and then reverse array: `arr = arr[::-1]` if reverse. But reversing is same score. Include both no need. Another variant with p>0.5 low first? We can sample p in [0.2,0.4]. For p>0.5, formula roles? To keep simple, use p<=0.5 and reversed.

`make_weights`: Need `c` nonnegative. `temp` based on difference top to median.
```
def make_weights(c, rng):
    M=np.max(c)
    if not finite: return None
    p50=np.percentile(c,50)
    temp=max(1e-12, (M-p50)*0.25 + 1e-12)
    w=np.exp((c-M)/temp)
    if rng.random()<0.5:
       # use only top mass
       thresh=np.quantile(c, rng.uniform(0.0,0.3))
       w[c<thresh]=0.0
    w *= (0.5 + 0.5*rng.random(c.size))
    s=w.sum()
    if s<=0: w=np.ones_like(c); s=c.size
    return w/s
```
Quantile with replace random each time maybe. The weights need nonnegative; sum1.

`linprog` method: Try 'highs', if not available? SciPy >1.7. We'll handle exceptions and attempt 'interior-point'.
```
def solve_lp(A, M, rng):
   if not HAVE_SOLVER or A.shape[1]==0: return None
   n=A.shape[1]
   r = rng.random(n) * 1e-5
   c = -(1.0 + r)
   b = np.full(A.shape[0], M, dtype=float)
   for method in ('highs','interior-point'):
       try:
           res = linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=(0.0,1000.0), method=method, options={'presolve': True})
           if res.success and res.x is not None:
               g = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=float)
               g[~np.isfinite(g)] = 0
               return np.clip(g,0,1000)
       except Exception:
           pass
   return None
```
If linprog fails due to max iterations but returns x? res.success false. Could still use `res.x` if finite? Maybe. We can check `hasattr(res,'x')`. If finite and sum>0, return. But constraints may violated; safe check later. Let's do:
```

    g=...
    # quick feasibility within A? if np.max(A@g) <= M*1+? else maybe return
    return g if feasible enough? 
```

def sota_run(f0, max_time, deadline, full_limit=900, active_L=384, rng=None):
   if rng is None: rng=np.random.default_rng()
   n=f0.size
   if n<2: return f0, inf
   S=float(np.sqrt(2.0*n))
   f=np.asarray(f0,float).copy()
   f=np.clip(f,0,1000.0)
   if f.sum()<=0: f=np.ones(n); f=f*S/n
   f=f*S/f.sum()
   best=f.copy(); bestM=max_conv(f)
   start=time.monotonic()
   it=0; no_imp=0
   last_lp_time=time.monotonic()
   while time.monotonic()<deadline and time.monotonic()-start<max_time:
      c=convolve_full(f); M=float(np.max(c))
      if M < bestM - 1e-12:
         best=f.copy(); bestM=M; no_imp=0
      else:
         no_imp += 1
      # Gradient attempts
      grad_done=0
      while grad_done < 3 and time...:
         w=make_weights(c,rng)
         cand=gradient_step(f,w,M)
         if cand is None: break
         cm=max_conv(cand)
         if cm < M - 1e-12:
            f=cand; c=convolve_full(f)? M=cm; grad_done=1
            if M<bestM: best=f.copy(); bestM=M; no_imp=0
            else: grad_done=3? 
         else:
            break
      if time... break
      # LP attempt every 2 iterations or if no_imp>2
      if HAVE_SOLVER and (it % 2 == 0 or no_imp>2):
         if n <= full_limit:
            row_idx=np.arange(2*n-1)
         else:
            row_idx=choose_active_rows(c,n,active_L,rng)
         improved_lp=False
         for mf in (1.0,0.92,0.85):
            A=build_active_matrix(f,row_idx)
            g=solve_lp(A,M*mf,rng)
            it_lp? 
            if g is None: continue
            sg=float(g.sum())
            if sg <= 1.01*S: continue
            fg=convolve_full(f,g)
            maxfg=float(np.max(fg))
            if maxfg > (M/S)*sg*(1+1e-7):
                continue
            g=nor(g,S)
            if np.any(g>1000): continue
            best_cm=M; best_tau=-1; best_cand=None
            taus=(0.05,0.1,0.2,0.35,0.5,0.75,1.0)
            for tau in taus:
                cand=(1-tau)*f+tau*g
                # maybe if tau==1 cand=g
                cand=np.clip(cand,0,1000)
                if cand.sum()<=0: continue
                cand=cand*S/cand.sum()
                cm=max_conv(cand)
                if cm < best_cm - 1e-10:
                   best_cm=cm; best_tau=tau; best_cand=cand
            if best_cand is not None:
                f=best_cand; M=best_cm; improved_lp=True; no_imp=0
                if M<bestM: best=f.copy(); bestM=M
                break
         if not improved_lp:
             # small perturb sometimes
             if no_imp > 3 and time...:
                cand=random_perturb(f,0.15,rng)
                cm=max_conv(cand)
                if cm < bestM*1.02: f=cand
                else: f=best.copy()
                no_imp=0
      # if no LP solver, do more gradient
      it +=1
   return best,bestM
```

`gradient_step` with current M:
```
def gradient_step(f, w, currentM):
    S=sqrt(2n)
    B=compute_B_fft(f,w)
    Bc=B-B.mean()
    std=Bc.std()
    if std < 1e-15: return None
    for eta in (0.5/std, 1.0/std, 2.0/std, 4.0/std):
       step=np.exp(-eta*Bc)
       step=np.clip(step, 1e-8, 1e6)
       cand=f*step
       cand=np.clip(cand,0,1000)
       s=cand.sum(); if s<=0 continue
       cand*=S/s
       cm=max_conv(cand)
       if cm < currentM - 1e-12: return cand
    return None
```
This evaluates multiple candidates; okay.

But `make_weights` uses c from previous f; after gradient update, c changed; we can recompute in loop. Good.

`random_perturb`:
```
def random_perturb(f, scale, rng):
   n=f.size
   cand=f.copy()
   width=int(rng.integers(max(2,n//100), max(3,n//5)))
   start=int(rng.integers(0,max(1,n-width+1)))
   cand[start:start+width] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,scale)))
   if rng.random()<0.25:
      cand=0.5*(cand+cand[::-1])
   # floor zero
   cand=np.where(cand>0,cand,0)
   s=cand.sum()
   if s<=0: cand=f.copy(); s=f.sum()
   S=sqrt(2n); return cand*S/s
```

`refine_step` can call `gradient_step` but gradient_step operates on full array; if we project after, better custom:
```
def set_block_means(a, edges):
   for k in range(len(edges)-1):
      s=edges[k]; e=edges[k+1]
      if e>s: a[s:e]=a[s:e].mean()
```
Top-level.

def refine_step(frac, n, rng, steps, deadline, init_style=0):
  K=len(frac)
  # edges
  cum=np.concatenate(([0.0], np.cumsum(frac)))
  edges=np.floor(cum*n).astype(int)
  edges[0]=0; edges[-1]=n
  # h init style
  h=init_heights(K, init_style, rng)
  best=None; bestM=inf
  for it in range(steps):
     if time.monotonic()>deadline: break
     a=make_step_from_edges(n, edges, h)
     a=a*S/a.sum()
     c=conv; M=max
     if M<bestM: best=a.copy(); bestM=M
     if it % 10 == 9 and bestM < 1.55: break
     w=make_weights(c,rng)
     B=compute_B_fft(a,w)
     Bc=B-B.mean(); std=Bc.std()
     if std<1e-15:
        h *= np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.1,K))
        continue
     eta=1.0/std
     step=np.exp(-eta*Bc); step=np.clip(step,0.05,20)
     a=a*step; np.clip
     set_block_means(a,edges)
     s=a.sum(); if s>0: a*=S/s
  return best,bestM
```

Upsampling:
```
def resample_sequence(a, new_n):
   n=a.size
   if n==new_n: return a
   x_old=np.arange(n); x_new=np.linspace(0,n-1,new_n)
   b=np.interp(x_new,x_old,a)
   b=np.clip(b,0,1000)
   if b.sum()<=0: b=np.ones(new_n)
   S=sqrt(2*new_n); return b*S/b.sum()
```
Interpolating a sequence with sharp steps smooths; may reduce discrete artifacts. Good.

`normalize_vector(v,S)`:

def normalize_vector(v,S):
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64)
    v = np.nan_to_num(v, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    v = np.clip(v, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(v.sum())
    if s <= 0 or not np.isfinite(s):
         v = np.ones(v.size, dtype=np.float64)
         s = float(v.size)
    v = v * (float(S)/s)
    # if cap caused sum change? If max exceeded 1000, renormalize? We clipped before scale; after scale might exceed 1000 if S/s large? S/s <=? S~45, elements maybe 1, scaling to sum could make if one element >? If original max small. To be safe:
    v = np.clip(v, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s2=v.sum()
    if s2>0: v=v*(S/s2) else v=...
    return v
```

`score_array` uses `np.max(c)` but if n large and c length, okay. If `a` contains no finite? `np.isfinite` check.
```
def score_array(a):
   a=np.asarray(a,float)
   if a.size==0: return inf
   a=np.clip(np.nan_to_num(...),0,1000)
   s=a.sum()
   if s<1e-8: return inf
   c=convolve_full(a)
   m=float(np.max(c))
   if not finite: return inf
   return float(2*a.size*m/(s*s))
```

      sc=score_array(a)
      if finite: items.append((sc,a))
   items.sort(key=candidate_key)
   return items[:k]
```

`init_heights`:
```
def init_heights(K, style, rng):
   i=np.arange(K,dtype=float)
   if style==0: h=np.ones(K)
   elif style==1: h=np.exp(rng.uniform(-0.8,0.8)*i)
   elif style==2: h=1.0 + 0.9*np.sin(np.pi*(i+1)/(K+1))
   elif style==3: h=np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.35,K))
   else: h=rng.lognormal(0,0.5,K)
   h=np.clip(h,1e-4,None)
   return h/h.sum()
```

`set_block_means(a, edges)` top-level.

`make_step_from_edges(n, edges, h)`:
```
a=np.empty(n)
for k in range(len(edges)-1):
   s=edges[k]; e=edges[k+1]
   if e>s: a[s:e]=h[k]
return a
```

`refine_step` uses edges from `frac`; h positive. It must be top-level.

```
def refine_step(frac, n, rng, steps, deadline, style=0):
   frac=np.asarray(frac,float); frac=np.clip(frac,1e-8,None); frac/=frac.sum()
   cum=np.concatenate(([0.0], np.cumsum(frac)))
   edges=np.floor(cum*n).astype(np.int64)
   edges[0]=0; edges[-1]=n
   K=len(frac)
   h=init_heights(K,style,rng)
   S=np.sqrt(2.0*n)
   best=None; bestM=np.inf
   a=make_step_from_edges(n,edges,h); a=normalize_vector(a,S)
   for it in range(steps):
      if time.monotonic() > deadline: break
      c=convolve_full(a); M=float(np.max(c))
      if M < bestM:
         bestM=M; best=a.copy()
      if M < 1.45: break
      w=make_weights(c,rng)
      B=compute_B_fft(a,w)
      Bc=B-B.mean(); std=float(Bc.std())
      if std <= 1e-15:
         h = h * np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.1,K))
         a=make_step_from_edges(n,edges,h); a=normalize_vector(a,S); continue
      for eta in (1.0/std, 0.5/std):
         step=np.exp(-eta*Bc)
         step=np.clip(step,0.05,20.0)
         cand=a*step; cand=np.clip(cand,0,1000); s=cand.sum()
         if s<=0: continue
         cand=cand*(S/s)
         # project to block
         cand_set=False
         for k in range(K):
             s0=edges[k]; e0=edges[k+1]
             if e0>s0:
                 cand[s0:e0]=cand[s0:e0].mean()
         s2=cand.sum(); if s2>0: cand=cand*(S/s2)
         cm=float(np.max(convolve_full(cand)))
         if cm < M - 1e-12:
             a=cand
             # update h from a
             for k in range(K):
                 s0=edges[k]; e0=edges[k+1]
                 h[k]=a[s0:e0].mean() if e0>s0 else h[k]
             h=np.clip(h,1e-4,None)
             break
   return best,bestM
```
Potential: After projecting, M might increase? We accept if cm<M. Good.

```
def choose_active_rows(c,n,L,rng):
   K=c.size
   if L>=K: return np.arange(K)
   top_n=max(1,int(L*0.7))
   top=np.argpartition(c, K-top_n)[-top_n:]  # faster, order arbitrary
   # include top by actual sort for top_n maybe
   top=top[np.argsort(c[top])[::-1]]
   rem=L-top_n
   M=np.max(c)
   temp=max(1e-12, M - np.median(c))
   probs=np.exp((c-M)/temp)
   s=probs.sum(); if s<=0: probs=np.ones(K)/K else probs/=s
   rand=rng.choice(K, rem, replace=False, p=probs)
   idx=np.concatenate((top,rand)); idx=np.unique(idx)
   if idx.size>L: idx=idx[:L]
   return idx
```
`np.argpartition` may fail if top_n==K; handle. Ensure `rem>0`. Good.

`make_weights` uses `np.quantile` maybe; use median.
```
def make_weights(c,rng):
   M=np.max(c)
   if not np.isfinite(M): return np.ones(c.size)/c.size
   base=np.median(c)
   temp=max(1e-12, (M-base)*0.2 + 1e-12)
   w=np.exp((c-M)/temp)
   # random sharpness
   sharp=rng.uniform(0.5,3.0)
   w = w ** sharp
   if rng.random()<0.3:
       thresh = np.linspace(0,M, num=max(2,int(c.size//10)))[0]? Maybe use quantile.
       thresh = np.percentile(c, rng.uniform(0.0,20.0))
       w[c < thresh] = 0.0
   w *= (0.5 + 0.5*rng.random(c.size))
   s=float(w.sum())
   if s<=0: w=np.ones_like(c); s=float(c.size)
   return w/s
```
`w**sharp` for w in [0,1] keeps top.

def random_initial_candidates(n, rng, k=8):

      add
   sort and return top k

def add_candidate(items, arr, score=None):

    if arr.size==0: return

def search_step_points(n, rng, deadline, items, max_candidates=6):
    Ks=(3,4,6,8)
    count=0
    for K in Ks:
       if time.monotonic()>deadline: break
       # fractions:
       fracs=[]
       fracs.append(np.ones(K)/K)
       for _ in range(2): fracs.append(rng.dirichlet(np.ones(K)*0.5))
       for frac in fracs:
          if time.monotonic()>deadline: break
          for style in (1,2):
             a,sc = refine_step(frac,n,rng,steps=70,deadline=time.monotonic()+15,style=style)
             if a is not None: add_candidate(items,a,sc)
             count+=1
             if count>=max_candidates: return
```

`resample_sequence` code:
```
def resample_sequence(a, new_n):
   a=np.asarray(a,float)
   n=a.size
   if n==new_n: return normalize_vector(a, sqrt(2*new_n))
   if n<2: return np.full(new_n, a[0])
   x_old=np.arange(n,dtype=float)
   x_new=np.linspace(0.0,n-1.0,new_n)
   b=np.interp(x_new,x_old,a)
   return normalize_vector(b, np.sqrt(2.0*new_n))
```

def add_candidate(items, arr, score=None):
    arr = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64)
    if arr.size == 0:
        return
    arr = np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if float(arr.sum()) <= 0.0:
        return
    if score is None or not np.isfinite(score):
        score = score_array(arr)
    if not np.isfinite(score):
        return
    items.append((float(score), arr.copy()))
    if len(items) > 16:
        items.sort(key=candidate_key)
        items.pop()
```
Popping worst after sort ascending removes last. Good.

```
def standardize_vector(v, S):
    v=np.asarray(v,float)
    v=np.nan_to_num(...)
    v=np.clip(v,0,1000)
    eps=max(1e-12, 1e-12*float(S))
    v=np.maximum(v, eps)
    s=float(v.sum())
    if s<=0 or not finite:
        v=np.ones_like(v); s=float(v.size)
    v=v*(float(S)/s)
    v=np.clip(v, eps, 1000.0)
    s=float(v.sum())
    if s>0: v=v*(S/s)
    return v
```
Use in sota. It floors all zeros. For final output, maybe no need but okay. The score changes by 1e-12 negligible.

Let's write `sota_run` carefully.

```
def sota_run(f0, max_time, deadline, full_limit=900, active_L=384, rng=None, do_lp=True):
    if rng is None: rng=np.random.default_rng()
    f=np.asarray(f0,dtype=np.float64)
    if f.ndim!=1 or f.size<2:
        f=np.ones(2)
    n=f.size
    S=float(np.sqrt(2.0*n))
    f=standardize_vector(f,S)
    best=f.copy()
    bestM=score_array(f)
    start=time.monotonic()
    it=0; no_imp=0
    while time.monotonic()<deadline and (time.monotonic()-start)<max_time:
        c=convolve_full(f); M=float(np.max(c))
        if M < bestM - 1e-12:
            best=f.copy(); bestM=M; no_imp=0
        else:
            no_imp += 1
        changed=False
        for _ in range(4):
            w=make_weights(c,rng)
            cand,cm=gradient_step(f,w,M)
            if cand is None:
                break
            f=standardize_vector(cand,S)
            c=convolve_full(f); M=float(np.max(c))
            changed=True
            if M < bestM -1e-12:
                best=f.copy(); bestM=M; no_imp=0
        if not do_lp or (not HAVE_SOLVER) or (it % 3 != 0 and no_imp <= 1):
            if no_imp>10:
               # perturb
               cand=random_perturb(f,0.2,rng)
               sc=score_array(cand)
               if sc < bestM*1.05:
                  f=standardize_vector(cand,S); c=convolve_full(f); M=float(np.max(c))
               no_imp=0
            it +=1
            continue
        # LP
        if n <= full_limit:
           row_idx=np.arange(2*n-1)
        else:
           L=min(active_L, 2*n-1)
           row_idx=choose_active_rows(c,n,L,rng)
        improved=False
        for mf in (1.0,0.9,0.8):
           if time.monotonic()>deadline: break
           A=build_active_matrix(f,row_idx)
           if A.size==0: continue
           g=solve_lp(A, M*float(mf), rng)
           if g is None: continue
           sg=float(np.sum(g))
           if sg <= 1.02*S: continue
           fg=convolve_full(f,g)
           maxfg=float(np.max(fg))
           if maxfg > (M / S) * sg * (1.0+1e-6) + 1e-9:
               continue
           g=standardize_vector(g*S/sg, S)
           if float(np.max(g)) > 1000.0:
               continue
           best_cm=M; best_cand=None; best_tau=-1
           taus=(0.05,0.1,0.2,0.35,0.5,0.75,1.0)
           for tau in taus:
              cand=(1.0-float(tau))*f + float(tau)*g
              cand=np.clip(cand,0.0,1000.0)
              if float(cand.sum())<=0: continue
              cand=standardize_vector(cand,S)
              cm=float(max_conv(cand))
              if cm < best_cm - 1e-10:
                 best_cm=cm; best_cand=cand; best_tau=float(tau)
           if best_cand is not None:
              f=best_cand; M=best_cm; improved=True; no_imp=0
              if M < bestM -1e-12:
                 best=f.copy(); bestM=M
              break
        if not improved and no_imp >= 8:
            cand=random_perturb(f,0.15,rng)
            sc=score_array(cand)
            if sc < bestM*1.03:
                f=standardize_vector(cand,S)
            else:
                f=best.copy()
            no_imp=0
        it +=1
    final_score=score_array(best)
    if bestM > final_score: # standardize
       bestM=final_score
    return best, bestM
```
Tuples `taus` okay. `(through)`. Note if no LP but no_imp logic: we only do LP if `do_lp and HAVE_SOLVER and (it % 3 == 0 or no_imp>1)`. If no solver, condition false, so continue with perturb. Good.

But direct `continue` increments it. Fine.

In `random_perturb`, need standardize? return normalized S? We'll define returns vector normalized to sqrt(2n). Use `S` internal.

`random_perturb`:
```
def random_perturb(f, scale, rng):
    n=f.size; S=np.sqrt(2*n)
    cand=f.copy()
    width=...
    if width > n: width=n
    start=int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n-width+1)))
    cand[start:start+width] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, scale))
    if rng.random()<0.25:
       cand=0.5*(cand+cand[::-1])
    cand=np.clip(cand,0,1000)
    if cand.sum()<=0: cand=f.copy()
    return standardize_vector(cand,S)
```

Now, `solve_lp` with `linprog` options. In SciPy, `linprog` `options` for highs may not accept `dual_feasibility_tolerance`? It accepts generic. To be safe, only `options={'presolve': True}`. If method中心医院? Good.
```
def solve_lp(A, M, rng):
    if not HAVE_SOLVER or A.shape[1]<=0:
       return None
    n=A.shape[1]
    r=1e-5*rng.random(n)
    c=-(1.0+r)
    b=np.full(A.shape[0], float(M), dtype=np.float64)
    for method in ('highs','interior-point'):
       try:
          res=linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=(0.0, 1000.0), method=method, options={'presolve': True})
          x=getattr(res,'x',None)
          if x is None: continue
          x=np.asarray(x,dtype=np.float64)
          if np.all(np.isfinite(x)):
             x=np.clip(x,0.0,1000.0)
             return x
       except Exception:
          pass
    return None
```
If `linprog` warns? okay.

Potential if A large and b too small? Feasible x=0. Fine.

probs=np.exp((c-M)/temp)
s=float(probs.sum())
if s<=0 or not finite: probs=np.ones(K)/K else probs/=s
```

def run_pool(items, pool_name, deadline, active_L, full_limit, rng, field):
   tops=top_k_candidates(pool,3)
   now=time.monotonic()
   left=deadline-now
   if left<=0: return
   per=left/max(1,len(tops))
   per=min(per, 300.0)
   for sc,a in tops:
      if time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
      b,sc=sota_run(a, max_time=per, deadline=deadline, full_limit=full_limit, active_L=active_L, rng=rng, do_lp=True)
      add_candidate(items,b,sc)
```

```
def run_pool(global_items, pool, deadline, full_limit, active_L, rng):
    tops = top_k_candidates(pool, 3)
    for _ in range(len(tops)):
       if time.monotonic() > deadline: break
       a = tops[_][1]
       left = deadline - time.monotonic()
       # for first top spend more? per=left/(remaining)
       rem = len(tops)-_
       per = min(300.0, max(60.0, left / max(1, rem)))
       b,sc = sota_run(a, max_time=per, deadline=deadline, full_limit=full_limit, active_L=active_L, rng=rng, do_lp=True)
       add_candidate(global_items,b,sc)
```

```python
"""
Search for low-max self-convolution nonnegative step sequences.

Method:
- Start from several constructive candidates (two-block, step functions, smooth random).
- Refine them with smooth convex-like gradient flows and randomized coordinate updates.
- Use the known LP fixed-point idea: for fixed f, maximize sum(g) subject to
  f*g <= ||f*f||_inf, then mix f toward the rescaled optimizer g.
- Run this at several resolutions and resample the best sequence.
"""

import numpy as np
import time

try:
    from scipy.optimize import linprog
    HAVE_SOLVER = True
except Exception:
    HAVE_SOLVER = False


def candidate_key(item):
    return item[0]


def next_pow2(m):
    p = 1
    while p < int(m):
        p <<= 1
    return p


def convolve_full(a, b=None):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    if b is None:
        b = a
    else:
        b = np.asarray(b, dtype=np.float64)
    L = 2 * n - 1
    nfft = next_pow2(L)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, nfft)
    fb = np.fft.rfft(b, nfft)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fb, nfft)[:L]
    c = np.nan_to_num(c, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    c[c < 0.0] = 0.0
    return c


def max_conv(a):
    c = convolve_full(a)
    if c.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return float("inf")
    return m


def score_array(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s <= 1e-12 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return float("inf")
    m = max_conv(a)
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return float("inf")
    return float(2.0 * a.size * m / (s * s))


def standardize_vector(v, S):
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64)
    if v.size == 0:
        return v
    v = np.nan_to_num(v, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    v = np.clip(v, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(v))
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        v = np.ones(v.size, dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(v.size)
    v = v * (float(S) / s)
    eps = max(1e-12, 1e-12 * float(S))
    for _ in range(3):
        v = np.clip(v, 0.0, 1000.0)
        v = np.maximum(v, eps)
        s = float(np.sum(v))
        if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
            v = np.ones(v.size, dtype=np.float64)
            s = float(v.size)
        v = v * (float(S) / s)
    v = np.clip(v, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(v))
    if s <= 0.0:
        v = np.ones(v.size, dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(v.size)
    v = v * (float(S) / s)
    return v


def normalize_vector(v, S):
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64)
    v = np.nan_to_num(v, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    v = np.clip(v, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(v))
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        v = np.ones(v.size, dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(v.size)
    v = v * (float(S) / s)
    v = np.clip(v, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(v))
    if s > 0.0 and np.isfinite(s):
        v = v * (float(S) / s)
    return v


def add_candidate(items, arr, score=None):
    arr = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64)
    if arr.size == 0:
        return
    arr = np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if float(np.sum(arr)) <= 0.0:
        return
    if score is None or not np.isfinite(score):
        score = score_array(arr)
    if not np.isfinite(score):
        return
    items.append((float(score), arr.copy()))
    if len(items) > 16:
        items.sort(key=candidate_key)
        items.pop()


def top_k_candidates(items, k):
    tmp = items[:]
    tmp.sort(key=candidate_key)
    return tmp[:max(0, int(k))]


def make_two_block(n, p, reverse=False):
    p = float(min(max(p, 0.02), 0.48))
    n = int(n)
    left = int(round(p * n))
    left = min(max(left, 1), max(1, n - 1))
    a = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    a_low = 1.0 / (1.0 + p)
    a_high = 1.0 / ((1.0 + p) * (1.0 - p))
    a[:left] = a_low
    a[left:] = a_high
    if reverse:
        a = a[::-1].copy()
    return a


def make_step_sequence(n, frac, height):
    n = int(n)
    frac = np.asarray(frac, dtype=np.float64)
    frac = np.nan_to_num(frac, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    frac = np.clip(frac, 1e-12, None)
    fs = float(frac.sum())
    if fs <= 0.0:
        frac = np.ones(frac.size, dtype=np.float64) / max(1, frac.size)
        fs = float(frac.sum())
    frac = frac / fs

    h = np.asarray(height, dtype=np.float64)
    h = np.nan_to_num(h, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    h = np.clip(h, 1e-12, None)
    if h.size != frac.size:
        h = np.ones(frac.size, dtype=np.float64)
    hs = float(h.sum())
    if hs <= 0.0:
        h = np.ones(frac.size, dtype=np.float64)

    K = int(frac.size)
    cum = np.empty(K + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    cum[0] = 0.0
    cum[1:] = np.cumsum(frac) * float(n)
    idx = np.searchsorted(cum, np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64), side="right")
    idx = np.minimum(idx, K - 1)
    a = h[idx]
    return a


def init_heights(K, style, rng):
    K = int(max(1, K))
    i = np.arange(K, dtype=np.float64)
    if style == 0:
        h = np.ones(K, dtype=np.float64)
    elif style == 1:
        h = np.exp(rng.uniform(-0.9, 0.9) * i)
    elif style == 2:
        h = 1.0 + 0.85 * np.sin(np.pi * (i + 1.0) / (K + 1.0))
    elif style == 3:
        h = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.35, K))
    else:
        h = rng.lognormal(0.0, 0.5, K)
    h = np.nan_to_num(h, nan=1.0, posinf=1.0, neginf=0.0)
    h = np.clip(h, 1e-4, None)
    hs = float(h.sum())
    if hs <= 0.0:
        h = np.ones(K, dtype=np.float64)
    return h / hs


def random_initial_candidates(n, rng, k=8):
    items = []
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 1 or rng is None:
        return items

    for p in (1.0 / 3.0, 0.27, 0.33, 0.37, 0.43, 0.47):
        a = make_two_block(n, p, False)
        add_candidate(items, a, None)
        add_candidate(items, a[::-1].copy(), None)

    for K in (2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 10):
        for style in (0, 1, 2, 3):
            for _ in range(2):
                if K == 2:
                    p = float(rng.uniform(0.22, 0.46))
                    frac = np.array([p, 1.0 - p])
                else:
                    frac = rng.dirichlet(np.ones(K) * 0.55)
                h = init_heights(K, style, rng)
                a = make_step_sequence(n, frac, h)
                add_candidate(items, a, None)
                add_candidate(items, a[::-1].copy(), None)

    for _ in range(5):
        x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
        f1 = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 1.0))
        f2 = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 1.0))
        amp = float(rng.uniform(0.45, 1.15))
        a = np.exp(amp * (np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * f1 * x + f2)
                          + 0.45 * np.cos(4.0 * np.pi * f1 * x + 2.0 * f2)))
        add_candidate(items, a, None)
        add_candidate(items, a[::-1].copy(), None)

    items.sort(key=candidate_key)
    return items[:max(0, int(k))]


def set_block_means(a, edges):
    for k in range(len(edges) - 1):
        s = int(edges[k])
        e = int(edges[k + 1])
        if e > s:
            a[s:e] = float(np.mean(a[s:e]))


def make_step_from_edges(n, edges, h):
    n = int(n)
    a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for k in range(len(edges) - 1):
        s = int(edges[k])
        e = int(edges[k + 1])
        if e > s:
            a[s:e] = float(h[k])
    return a


def refine_step(frac, n, rng, steps, deadline, style=0):
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 1:
        return make_two_block(n, 0.4), score_array(make_two_block(n, 0.4))

    frac = np.asarray(frac, dtype=np.float64)
    frac = np.nan_to_num(frac, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    frac = np.clip(frac, 1e-8, None)
    fsum = float(frac.sum())
    if fsum <= 0.0:
        frac = np.ones(max(1, frac.size), dtype=np.float64)
        fsum = float(frac.size)
    frac = frac / fsum

    cum = np.empty(frac.size + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    cum[0] = 0.0
    cum[1:] = np.cumsum(frac) * float(n)
    edges = np.floor(cum).astype(np.int64)
    edges[0] = 0
    edges[-1] = n
    K = int(frac.size)

    h = init_heights(K, style, rng)
    S = float(np.sqrt(2.0 * n))
    a = make_step_from_edges(n, edges, h)
    a = standardize_vector(a, S)

    best = a.copy()
    bestM = float(np.max(convolve_full(a)))

    for it in range(int(max(0, steps))):
        if time.monotonic() > float(deadline):
            break

        c = convolve_full(a)
        M = float(np.max(c))
        if M < bestM:
            bestM = M
            best = a.copy()
        if bestM <= 1.45:
            break

        if it % 12 == 11:
            h *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.06, K))
            h = np.clip(h, 1e-4, None)
            a = make_step_from_edges(n, edges, h)
            a = standardize_vector(a, S)
            continue

        w = make_weights(c, rng)
        B = compute_B_fft(a, w)
        Bc = B - float(np.mean(B))
        std = float(np.std(Bc))
        if std <= 1e-14:
            h *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.2, K))
            h = np.clip(h, 1e-4, None)
            a = make_step_from_edges(n, edges, h)
            a = standardize_vector(a, S)
            continue

        improved = False
        for eta in (1.0 / std, 0.5 / std, 2.0 / std):
            Bc_clip = np.clip(Bc, -3.0 * std, 3.0 * std)
            step = np.exp(-float(eta) * Bc_clip)
            step = np.clip(step, 1e-4, 1e4)
            cand = a * step
            cand = np.clip(cand, 0.0, 1000.0)
            s = float(np.sum(cand))
            if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
                continue
            cand = cand * (S / s)
            set_block_means(cand, edges)
            cand = standardize_vector(cand, S)
            cm = float(np.max(convolve_full(cand)))
            if cm < M - 1e-12:
                a = cand
                for kk in range(K):
                    s0 = int(edges[kk])
                    e0 = int(edges[kk + 1])
                    if e0 > s0:
                        h[kk] = float(np.mean(a[s0:e0]))
                h = np.clip(h, 1e-4, None)
                M = cm
                improved = True
                break

        if not improved and it % 7 == 6:
            a = random_perturb(a, 0.18, rng)

    return best, bestM


def make_weights(c, rng):
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    K = c.size
    if K == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    M = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(M):
        return np.ones(K, dtype=np.float64) / float(K)
    base = float(np.median(c))
    temp = max(1e-12, 0.25 * (M - base) + 1e-12)
    w = np.exp((c - M) / temp)
    sharp = float(rng.uniform(0.7, 2.8))
    with np.errstate(over="ignore", under="ignore", invalid="ignore"):
        w = w ** sharp
    w = np.nan_to_num(w, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)

    if rng.random() < 0.35:
        thresh = float(np.percentile(c, float(rng.uniform(0.0, 18.0))))
        w[c < thresh] = 0.0

    w *= 0.5 + 0.5 * rng.random(c.size)
    s = float(w.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        w = np.ones(K, dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(K)
    return w / s


def compute_B_fft(f, lam):
    f = np.asarray(f, dtype=np.float64)
    lam = np.asarray(lam, dtype=np.float64)
    n = f.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    L = next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    F = np.fft.rfft(f, L)
    W = np.fft.rfft(lam, L)
    B = np.fft.irfft(W * np.conj(F), L)[:n]
    B = np.nan_to_num(B, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    B[B < 0.0] = 0.0
    return B


def gradient_step(f, w, currentM):
    f = np.asarray(f, dtype=np.float64)
    n = f.size
    S = float(np.sqrt(2.0 * n))
    B = compute_B_fft(f, w)
    Bc = B - float(np.mean(B))
    std = float(np.std(Bc))
    if std <= 1e-14:
        return None, currentM

    best_cand = None
    best_score = currentM
    for eta in (0.7 / std, 1.0 / std, 1.6 / std, 2.8 / std):
        Bc_clip = np.clip(Bc, -3.0 * std, 3.0 * std)
        step = np.exp(-float(eta) * Bc_clip)
        step = np.clip(step, 1e-8, 1e8)
        cand = f * step
        cand = np.clip(cand, 0.0, 1000.0)
        cand = standardize_vector(cand, S)
        cm = float(np.max(convolve_full(cand)))
        if np.isfinite(cm) and cm < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_score = cm
            best_cand = cand
    return best_cand, best_score


def random_perturb(f, scale, rng):
    f = np.asarray(f, dtype=np.float64)
    n = f.size
    S = float(np.sqrt(2.0 * n))
    cand = f.copy()
    if n > 1 and scale > 0.0:
        lo = max(2, int(n // 120))
        hi = max(lo + 1, int(n // 6))
        width = int(rng.integers(lo, hi + 1))
        width = min(width, n)
        start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - width + 1)))
        cand[start:start + width] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, float(scale))))
        if rng.random() < 0.25:
            cand = 0.5 * (cand + cand[::-1])
    cand = np.clip(cand, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if float(np.sum(cand)) <= 0.0:
        cand = f.copy()
    return standardize_vector(cand, S)


def build_active_matrix(f, row_idx):
    f = np.asarray(f, dtype=np.float64)
    n = f.size
    row_idx = np.asarray(row_idx, dtype=np.int64)
    L = row_idx.size
    A = np.zeros((L, n), dtype=np.float64)
    for r, kk in enumerate(row_idx):
        kk = int(kk)
        if kk < 0 or kk > 2 * n - 2:
            continue
        s = max(0, kk - (n - 1))
        e = min(n - 1, kk)
        if s <= e:
            A[r, s:e + 1] = f[kk - e:kk - s + 1][::-1]
    return A


def choose_active_rows(c, n, L, rng):
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    K = c.size
    L = int(L)
    if L <= 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.int64)
    if L >= K:
        return np.arange(K, dtype=np.int64)

    top_n = max(1, int(0.75 * L))
    top_n = min(top_n, K)
    if top_n >= K:
        top = np.argsort(c)[::-1][:K]
    else:
        part = np.argpartition(c, K - top_n)[-top_n:]
        top = part[np.argsort(c[part])[::-1]]

    rem = L - top.size
    if rem > 0:
        M = float(np.max(c))
        temp = max(1e-12, M - float(np.median(c)))
        with np.errstate(over="ignore", under="ignore", invalid="ignore"):
            probs = np.exp((c - M) / temp)
        probs = np.nan_to_num(probs, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
        ps = float(probs.sum())
        if ps <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(ps):
            rand = rng.choice(K, size=rem, replace=False)
        else:
            probs = probs / ps
            rand = rng.choice(K, size=rem, replace=False, p=probs)
        idx = np.concatenate((top, rand))
    else:
        idx = top
    idx = np.unique(idx.astype(np.int64))
    return idx[:L]


def solve_lp(A, M, rng):
    if not HAVE_SOLVER:
        return None
    if A is None:
        return None
    n = int(A.shape[1])
    if n <= 0:
        return None

    r = 1e-5 * rng.random(n)
    c = -(1.0 + r)
    b = np.full(A.shape[0], float(M), dtype=np.float64)

    methods = ("highs", "interior-point")
    for method in methods:
        try:
            res = linprog(
                c,
                A_ub=A,
                b_ub=b,
                bounds=(0.0, 1000.0),
                method=method,
                options={"presolve": True},
            )
            x = getattr(res, "x", None)
            if x is None:
                continue
            x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
            if np.all(np.isfinite(x)):
                x = np.clip(x, 0.0, 1000.0)
                return x
        except Exception:
            pass
    return None


def sota_run(f0, max_time, deadline, full_limit=900, active_L=384, rng=None, do_lp=True):
    if rng is None:
        rng = np.random.default_rng()

    f = np.asarray(f0, dtype=np.float64)
    if f.ndim != 1 or f.size < 2:
        f = np.ones(2, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(f.size)
    S = float(np.sqrt(2.0 * n))
    f = standardize_vector(f, S)

    best = f.copy()
    bestM = score_array(best)
    start = time.monotonic()
    it = 0
    no_imp = 0

    while time.monotonic() < float(deadline) and (time.monotonic() - start) < float(max_time):
        c = convolve_full(f)
        M = float(np.max(c))
        if M < bestM - 1e-12:
            best = f.copy()
            bestM = M
            no_imp = 0
        else:
            no_imp += 1

        grad_changed = False
        for _ in range(4):
            w = make_weights(c, rng)
            cand, cm = gradient_step(f, w, M)
            if cand is None:
                break
            f = standardize_vector(cand, S)
            c = convolve_full(f)
            M = float(np.max(c))
            grad_changed = True
            if M < bestM - 1e-12:
                best = f.copy()
                bestM = M
                no_imp = 0

        use_lp = bool(do_lp and HAVE_SOLVER)
        if use_lp and (it % 3 == 0 or no_imp > 1):
            if n <= int(full_limit):
                row_idx = np.arange(2 * n - 1, dtype=np.int64)
            else:
                L = min(int(active_L), 2 * n - 1)
                row_idx = choose_active_rows(c, n, L, rng)

            improved = False
            for mf in (1.0, 0.9, 0.8):
                if time.monotonic() > float(deadline):
                    break
                A = build_active_matrix(f, row_idx)
                if A.size == 0:
                    continue
                g = solve_lp(A, M * float(mf), rng)
                if g is None:
                    continue
                sg = float(np.sum(g))
                if sg <= 1.02 * S:
                    continue

                fg = convolve_full(f, g)
                maxfg = float(np.max(fg))
                if maxfg > (M / S) * sg * (1.0 + 1e-6) + 1e-9:
                    continue

                g = standardize_vector(g * (S / sg), S)
                if float(np.max(g)) > 1000.0:
                    continue

                best_cm = M
                best_cand = None
                taus = (0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.35, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0)
                for tau in taus:
                    tau = float(tau)
                    cand = (1.0 - tau) * f + tau * g
                    cand = np.clip(cand, 0.0, 1000.0)
                    if float(np.sum(cand)) <= 0.0:
                        continue
                    cand = standardize_vector(cand, S)
                    cm = float(np.max(convolve_full(cand)))
                    if cm < best_cm - 1e-10:
                        best_cm = cm
                        best_cand = cand
                        best_tau = tau

                if best_cand is not None:
                    f = best_cand
                    M = best_cm
                    improved = True
                    no_imp = 0
                    if M < bestM - 1e-12:
                        best = f.copy()
                        bestM = M
                    break

            if not improved and no_imp >= 8:
                cand = random_perturb(f, 0.16, rng)
                sc = score_array(cand)
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < bestM * 1.03:
                    f = standardize_vector(cand, S)
                else:
                    f = best.copy()
                no_imp = 0
        else:
            if no_imp >= 10:
                cand = random_perturb(f, 0.20, rng)
                sc = score_array(cand)
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < bestM * 1.05:
                    f = standardize_vector(cand, S)
                else:
                    f = best.copy()
                no_imp = 0

        it += 1

    final_score = score_array(best)
    if np.isfinite(final_score) and final_score < bestM:
        bestM = final_score
    return best, bestM


def resample_sequence(a, new_n):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(a.size)
    new_n = int(new_n)
    if n <= 0 or new_n <= 0:
        return np.ones(new_n, dtype=np.float64)
    S = float(np.sqrt(2.0 * new_n))
    if n == new_n:
        return standardize_vector(a, S)
    if n == 1:
        val = float(a[0])
        return np.full(new_n, val, dtype=np.float64)

    x_old = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, float(n - 1), new_n)
    b = np.interp(x_new, x_old, a)
    b = np.nan_to_num(b, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    b = np.clip(b, 0.0, 1000.0)
    return standardize_vector(b, S)


def run_pool(global_items, pool, deadline, full_limit, active_L, rng):
    if not pool:
        return
    tops = top_k_candidates(pool, 3)
    count = len(tops)
    if count <= 0:
        return
    for idx, (_, a) in enumerate(tops):
        if time.monotonic() > float(deadline):
            break
        left = float(deadline) - time.monotonic()
        if left <= 1.0:
            break
        rem = max(1, count - idx)
        per = max(30.0, left / float(rem))
        per = min(per, 320.0)
        b, sc = sota_run(
            a,
            max_time=per,
            deadline=deadline,
            full_limit=full_limit,
            active_L=active_L,
            rng=rng,
            do_lp=HAVE_SOLVER,
        )
        add_candidate(global_items, b, sc)


def exact_score_list(seq):
    try:
        val = evaluate_sequence(seq)
    except Exception:
        val = np.inf
    return float(val)


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """Run global and local searches and return the best nonnegative sequence."""
    start = time.monotonic()
    total = max(120.0, float(budget_s))
    hard = start + max(30.0, total - 90.0)

    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed) if np.isfinite(seed) else 42)

    global_items = []
    n1_items = []
    n2_items = []

    parent = None
    try:
        p = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
        if p is not None:
            p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
            p = np.nan_to_num(p, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
            p = np.clip(p, 0.0, 1000.0)
            if p.size > 0 and float(np.sum(p)) > 0.0:
                parent = p
                add_candidate(global_items, parent, None)
    except Exception:
        parent = None

    # Work sizes.  Large enough for targets around 1.5, but still feasible with
    # dense reduced LPs and FFT convolution.
    N1 = 1024
    N2 = 2048
    N3 = 4096

    # Time budgets.
    if total >= 1500.0:
        t1 = start + 260.0
        t2 = start + 1050.0
        t3 = start + 1700.0
    elif total >= 800.0:
        t1 = start + 140.0
        t2 = start + 560.0
        t3 = start + 900.0
    else:
        t1 = start + max(20.0, 0.18 * total)
        t2 = start + max(50.0, 0.55 * total)
        t3 = start + max(20.0, 0.88 * total)

    t1 = min(t1, hard - 120.0)
    t2 = min(t2, hard - 120.0)
    t3 = min(t3, hard - 40.0)
    t4 = hard - 45.0

    # Initial constructions at small resolution.
    inits = random_initial_candidates(N1, rng, 12)
    for sc, a in inits:
        add_candidate(n1_items, a, sc)
        add_candidate(global_items, a, sc)

    # Short step-refinement phase.
    step_trials = 0
    max_step_trials = 12
    Ks = (2, 3, 4, 6, 8)
    for K in Ks:
        if time.monotonic() > t1 or step_trials >= max_step_trials:
            break
        fracs = []
        if K == 2:
            fracs.append(np.array([1.0 / 3.0, 2.0 / 3.0]))
            fracs.append(np.array([0.3, 0.7]))
            fracs.append(rng.dirichlet(np.ones(2) * 0.6))
        else:
            fracs.append(np.ones(K) / float(K))
            fracs.append(rng.dirichlet(np.ones(K) * 0.55))
            if time.monotonic() < t1:
                fracs.append(rng.dirichlet(np.ones(K) * 0.35))

        for frac in fracs:
            if time.monotonic() > t1 or step_trials >= max_step_trials:
                break
            for style in (1, 2, 3):
                if time.monotonic() > t1 or step_trials >= max_step_trials:
                    break
                a, sc = refine_step(
                    frac,
                    N1,
                    rng,
                    steps=60,
                    deadline=min(t1, time.monotonic() + 14.0),
                    style=style,
                )
                add_candidate(n1_items, a, sc)
                add_candidate(global_items, a, sc)
                step_trials += 1

    # Main LP/gradient refinement at N1.
    if time.monotonic() < t2:
        run_pool(global_items, n1_items, t2, 1000, 256, rng)

    # Build and refine at N2.
    if time.monotonic() < t3:
        for sc, a in top_k_candidates(n1_items, 4):
            b = resample_sequence(a, N2)
            add_candidate(n2_items, b, None)
            add_candidate(global_items, b, None)

        for p_val in (1.0 / 3.0, 0.35, 0.42):
            for rev in (False, True):
                a = make_two_block(N2, p_val, rev)
                a = standardize_vector(a, float(np.sqrt(2.0 * N2)))
                add_candidate(n2_items, a, None)
                add_candidate(global_items, a, None)

        # A small step refinement at N2 if time allows.
        if t3 - time.monotonic() > 40.0:
            step_deadline = min(t3 - 90.0, time.monotonic() + 80.0)
            for K, style in ((4, 1), (6, 2), (8, 3)):
                if time.monotonic() > step_deadline:
                    break
                frac = np.ones(K) / float(K) if K != 2 else np.array([1.0 / 3.0, 2.0 / 3.0])
                if K != 2:
                    frac = rng.dirichlet(np.ones(K) * 0.6)
                a, sc = refine_step(
                    frac,
                    N2,
                    rng,
                    steps=45,
                    deadline=step_deadline,
                    style=style,
                )
                add_candidate(n2_items, a, sc)
                add_candidate(global_items, a, sc)

        if time.monotonic() < t3:
            run_pool(global_items, n2_items, t3, 900, 512, rng)

    # Optional N3 and parent-length refinement.
    stage4_deadline = min(t4, hard - 30.0)
    if stage4_deadline > time.monotonic() + 60.0:
        # If a short sequence is good, polish it at N3.
        if top_k_candidates(n2_items, 1):
            a = resample_sequence(top_k_candidates(n2_items, 1)[0][1], N3)
            add_candidate(global_items, a, None)
            left = stage4_deadline - time.monotonic()
            if left > 90.0:
                b, sc = sota_run(
                    a,
                    max_time=min(220.0, left - 30.0),
                    deadline=stage4_deadline,
                    full_limit=900,
                    active_L=384,
                    rng=rng,
                    do_lp=HAVE_SOLVER,
                )
                add_candidate(global_items, b, sc)
                stage4_deadline = min(stage4_deadline, time.monotonic() + 30.0)

        # Try improving the supplied construction if it exists and is not huge.
        if parent is not None and stage4_deadline - time.monotonic() > 120.0:
            if parent.size <= 20000:
                left = stage4_deadline - time.monotonic()
                b, sc = sota_run(
                    parent,
                    max_time=min(260.0, left - 40.0),
                    deadline=stage4_deadline,
                    full_limit=900,
                    active_L=384,
                    rng=rng,
                    do_lp=HAVE_SOLVER,
                )
                add_candidate(global_items, b, sc)

    # Final cheap polish on the best known sequence.
    if global_items:
        global_items.sort(key=candidate_key)
        best_fft, best_arr = global_items[0][0], global_items[0][1]
        left = hard - time.monotonic()
        if left > 45.0:
            b, sc = sota_run(
                best_arr,
                max_time=min(120.0, left - 20.0),
                deadline=hard,
                full_limit=900,
                active_L=384,
                rng=rng,
                do_lp=(HAVE_SOLVER and best_arr.size <= 3000),
            )
            add_candidate(global_items, b, sc)
            global_items.sort(key=candidate_key)
            best_fft, best_arr = global_items[0][0], global_items[0][1]

    if not global_items:
        a = make_two_block(1024, 1.0 / 3.0, False)
        a = standardize_vector(a, float(np.sqrt(2048.0)))
        best_arr = a
        global_items.append((score_array(a), a))

    # Final exact evaluation of top few candidates if time remains.
    global_items.sort(key=candidate_key)
    unique = []
    for sc, arr in global_items:
        arr = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64)
        if arr.size == 0:
            continue
        arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
        ok = True
        for u in unique:
            if u.size == arr.size:
                # A cheap fingerprint avoids exact O(n) array_equal for many large arrays.
                z1 = arr[:: max(1, arr.size // 64)]
                z2 = u[:: max(1, u.size // 64)]
                if z1.size and np.allclose(z1, z2, rtol=1e-10, atol=1e-12):
                    ok = False
                    break
        if ok:
            unique.append(arr)
        if len(unique) >= 5:
            break

    best_seq = best_arr
    best_exact = float("inf")

    if time.monotonic() < start + total - 10.0:
        for arr in unique:
            if time.monotonic() > start + total - 8.0:
                break
            ex = exact_score_list(arr.tolist())
            if np.isfinite(ex) and ex < best_exact:
                best_exact = ex
                best_seq = arr

    # Fallback guarantee: a valid nonempty finite nonnegative list.
    if best_seq is None:
        best_seq = make_two_block(1024, 1.0 / 3.0, False)
        best_seq = standardize_vector(best_seq, float(np.sqrt(2048.0)))

    out = np.asarray(best_seq, dtype=np.float64)
    out = np.nan_to_num(out, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    out = np.clip(out, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(out))
    if s <= 0.0:
        out = np.ones(1024, dtype=np.float64)
    out = out.tolist()
    return out
```
